- **socratic**: Socratic tutoring — guides the learner to an answer through questions and never states it, not even when asked directly. - **socs-concept-building**: Use to extract concepts or mechanisms from empirical materials for 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — naming a phenomenon, defining it, bounding it, and moving back and forth between materials and sociological theory. Use especially for qualitative work that risks staying at the level of description. - **socs-fit-positioning**: Use to judge whether a manuscript fits 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — distinguishing a genuine sociological problematic from an economics-style policy-evaluation paper, and re-routing the latter to economics or cross-disciplinary journals. Use first, before drafting, when discipline fit is uncertain. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-fit-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-mechanism-social-process**: Use to translate coefficients or fieldwork materials into a social process for 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — specifying who, via what mechanism, under what structural conditions, forms what social outcome or structure. Use when results stop at significance or at a vivid story without an explicit mechanism. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-mechanism-social-process) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-problematic**: Use to build a sociological problem consciousness (问题意识) for 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — turning a variable relationship or a pile of fieldwork into a question about how a social mechanism, structure, or process works. Use when the manuscript still reads as "X affects Y" without a sociological stake. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-problematic) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-qualitative**: Use for the qualitative path in 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — fieldwork ethnography, in-depth interviews, the extended case method, or grounded theory. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-qualitative) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-quantitative**: Use for the quantitative path in 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — analyzing survey data (CGSS/CFPS/CLDS) with regression / Logit / multilevel / event-history (survival) / sequence analysis, and interpreting results as a sociological mechanism rather than entering an econometric identification arms race. Use when the draft only reports coefficients. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-quantitative) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-rebuttal**: Use to draft the response letter for an R&R at 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — addressing reviewer concerns about sociological problematic, theory dialogue, mechanism/social process, qualitative trustworthiness, or quantitative interpretation, point by point, with respect and evidence. Use only after the manuscript itself has been revised. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-rebuttal) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-style**: Use when polishing the language of a 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) manuscript — removing both policy-report tone (加强/完善/推进) and pure-technique tone (method-flexing), and restoring a scholarly sociological register with paragraph-leading topic sentences. Use after the problematic and mechanism are settled. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-style) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-submission**: Use for the pre-submission preflight to 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — checking in-text author-date citation (作者, 年: 页) plus end references (GB/T 7714, A–Z, 中外文分列), explanatory footnotes, abstract/keyword limits, the official online system (the journal's only channel), anonymity, and document hygiene. Verify every rule against the journal's current 投稿指南. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-submission) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-theory-dialogue**: Use to build genuine dialogue with classic and contemporary sociological theory for 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies) — entering a theoretical lineage (stratification, networks, institutions, culture, action) rather than piling up recent empirical citations. Use when the literature review is a variable list, not a conversation. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Studies-Skills/skills/socs-theory-dialogue) · ★1.1k)
- **socs-workflow**: Use when deciding which socs-* sub-skill to invoke next, or when sequencing manuscript work from problematic framing through rebuttal for 《社会学研究》 (Sociological Studies). - **soctheory-argument-development**: Use when stress-testing the reasoning of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript — turning stated propositions into a valid, warranted argument that survives rival theories and counter-cases. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-argument-development) · ★1.1k)
- **soctheory-boundary-conditions**: Use when specifying the scope, domain, and limits of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript's theory — stating where it holds, where it does not, and what it does NOT claim. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-boundary-conditions) · ★1.1k)

([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Sociological-Theory-Skills/skills/soctheory-conceptual-exhibits) · ★1.1k)
- **soctheory-contribution-framing**: Use when articulating the theoretical contribution of a Sociological Theory (ST) manuscript — naming the new way of seeing it provides and differentiating it from prior theory. - **soctheory-review-process**: Use when you need to understand Sociological Theory (ST)'s double-anonymous peer review and interpret a decision letter focused on conceptual novelty and logical soundness. - **solady-erc20-permit2-assumptions**: Detect unsafe assumptions about Solady's gas-optimized ERC20/ERC2612 permit and DN404 metadata. Solady's ERC20 uses custom storage slots, returns bools via assembly, exposes a virtual `_constantNameHash`/`_versionHash` for permit domain separation, and its DN404 mirror splits ERC20/ERC721 logic — integrators that assume OZ-style behavior, revert strings, or that `name()`/`decimals()` are always present can misbehave. Activate on solady/tokens imports, ERC2612 permit flows, DN404, or off-chain code parsing Solady revert reasons. - **solady-ownable-init-frontrun**: Detect front-runnable ownership initialization in Solady Ownable / OwnableRoles. Solady's `_initializeOwner` is a guarded one-time setter (it reverts with `AlreadyInitialized` on a second call) but it is NOT access-controlled, so in constructor-less deployment paths (minimal-proxy clones, EIP-1167, factory `create`/`create2` without atomic init) an attacker can call the public initializer first and seize ownership. Activate on solady Ownable/OwnableRoles in clones, factories, or any non-atomic deploy+init. ([iktok90-design/ai-smart-contract-auditor](https://github.com/iktok90-design/ai-smart-contract-auditor/tree/HEAD/rules/solady-gotchas/skills/solady-ownable-init-frontrun) · ★37 · 2 copies)
- **solady-safetransferlib-no-contract-check**: Detect Solady SafeTransferLib calls that assume the token has code. SafeTransferLib.safeTransfer/safeTransferFrom/safeApprove deliberately skip the EXTCODESIZE check that OpenZeppelin's SafeERC20 performs, so a call to an EOA or a self-destructed/not-yet-deployed token address returns success with no transfer.
